gty_beq

cjr.examples.generic_print.gty_beq

AI-generated explanation

Explanation unavailable: No matching cached explanation (offline mode).

recursive function gty_beq:
    parameter(a, b, type(gty))
    type:
        bool
    body:
        match RegularStyle:
            a
            b
            case:
                patterns(GTInt, GTInt)
                patterns(GTBool, GTBool)
                patterns(GTUnit, GTUnit)
                true
            case:
                patterns(GTVar(x), GTVar(y))
                String.eqb(x, y)
            case:
                patterns(GTNamed(n, ts), GTNamed(m, us))
                boolean_and:
                    String.eqb(n, m)
                    apply:
                        original Rocq [CFix]:
                            fix go ts us := match ts, us with
                                   | [], [] => true | t :: ts, u :: us => gty_beq t u && go ts us | _, _ => false end
                        ts
                        us
            case:
                patterns(GTArrow(a, b), GTArrow(c, d))
                boolean_and(gty_beq(a, c), gty_beq(b, d))
            case:
                patterns(GTBuffer(a), GTBuffer(b))
                gty_beq(a, b)
            case:
                patterns(_, _)
                false

Depends on

  • cjr.generics.gty

Project reference has no rendered declaration: cjr.examples.generic_print.gty_beq:3

Original declaration (proof-free)
Fixpoint gty_beq (a b : gty) : bool :=
  match a, b with
  | GTInt, GTInt | GTBool, GTBool | GTUnit, GTUnit => true
  | GTVar x, GTVar y => String.eqb x y
  | GTNamed n ts, GTNamed m us => String.eqb n m &&
      (fix go ts us := match ts, us with
       | [], [] => true | t :: ts, u :: us => gty_beq t u && go ts us | _, _ => false end) ts us
  | GTArrow a b, GTArrow c d => gty_beq a c && gty_beq b d
  | GTBuffer a, GTBuffer b => gty_beq a b
  | _, _ => false
  end.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

# Split CJR into three local repositories

## Summary

Create three new Git repositories under `/Users/danghica/cjr`:

- **cjr-core**: framework, extraction printers, shared development tools, `rocq_readable`, `misc`, and introductory generics.
- **cjr-collections**: arrays, array lists, polymorphic arrays, linked lists, list reversal, quicksort, most-frequent, hash maps, and quadtrees.
- **cjr-crypto**: AES encryption/decryption and IGE, plus the authenticator core, CLI, and macOS UI.

Each repository gets offline HTML documentation, a PDF tutorial, and runtime benchmarks. Skip proof validation throughout the migration.

## Preserve and separate

- Record outstanding exact human prompts using the existing logging script, without duplicate entries.
- First commit the current repository, including new source work, prompt history, and the existing verification log. Do not run proof validation or refresh that log.
- Initialize three fresh Git repositories and record the original checkpoint commit in their migration notes. Do not create remotes or push.
- Preserve source files, fixtures, extracted Cangjie, benchmark history, and required documentation assets; exclude compiled objects, binaries, and caches.
- After confirming migration completeness, remove the original working-tree copies from the parent repository. Retain a lightweight workspace guide, migration records, and historical logs. The checkpoint preserves the monolith.

## Dependencies and tooling

- Retain `example/<name>` within each child repository to reduce path changes.
- Collections depends on core; crypto depends on core and collections. Pin dependency commit IDs, use sibling checkouts by default, and support `CJR_CORE_DIR` and `CJR_COLLECTIONS_DIR` overrides.
- **Keep `rocq_readable` exclusively in cjr-core**, including its implementation, launcher, packaging, renderer resources, and tests. Collections and crypto invoke that copy through their core dependency; neither contains a copied or vendored renderer.
- Keep other shared development tooling and extraction printers in core. Make tooling accept the consuming repository’s root and example inventory.
- Keep arrays and their shared proof tactics in collections. Split extraction drivers and assumption audits by repository, removing circular dependencies and preserving logical module names where possible.
- Provide consistent `build`, `extract`, `test`, `verify`, `docs`, `paper`, `benchmark`, and `report` targets. Separate runtime and document tasks from proof compilation.

## Documentation and benchmarks

- Each repository owns its documentation content, configuration, generated HTML, and explanation caches. Give each a `docs/index.html` linking setup instructions, specifications, tests, benchmarks, and its PDF tutorial.
- Split the tutorial into three independently readable papers with editable LaTeX sources and `paper/tutorial.pdf` outputs. Add missing quadtree and authenticator explanations; preserve qualifications about proof coverage and target-language limitations.
- During migration, relocate existing specification HTML and caches, repair navigation, and avoid invoking proof-checking renderer backends. Future regeneration uses the renderer in core.
- Preserve historical benchmark measurements and provenance. Run fresh collections and AES benchmarks.
- Add core benchmarks for arithmetic, swap, block summation, and generic callbacks against equivalent handwritten Cangjie.
- Add authenticator benchmarks for SHA-1, HMAC, HOTP/TOTP, Base32 decoding, and account-session operations using deterministic synthetic inputs.
- Produce raw samples, medians/IQR, provenance, charts, and HTML reports. Generate each paper’s benchmark material from its repository’s results.

## Validation and completion

- Confirm every original source and fixture has a destination, including uncommitted generics work.
- Check that only core contains the `rocq_readable` implementation and that consumers resolve it through their pinned dependency.
- Run applicable tooling tests, Cangjie runtime tests, benchmark correctness checks, and frontend checks that do not trigger proof validation.
- Build and visually inspect all three PDFs; check HTML links, offline navigation, dependency resolution, and execution without the former monolithic layout.
- Do not run proof compilation, `coqchk`, assumption audits, or proof-checking documentation backends. Preserve existing frontend proof gates and report any validation they prevent.
- Commit each completed child repository and record its commit ID in the parent workspace.

Acceptance requires three separate repositories with complete HTML documentation, PDF tutorials, and reproducible runtime benchmark reports. Migration notes explicitly state that proofs were not revalidated.

recursive function distinct:
    parameter(ns, type(list(string)))
    type:
        bool
    body:
        match RegularStyle:
            ns
            case:
                patterns(empty_list)
                true
            case:
                patterns(cons(n, ns))
                boolean_and(negb(member(n, ns)), distinct(ns))

Depends on

  • cjr.examples.generic_print.distinct
  • cjr.examples.generic_print.member
Original declaration (proof-free)
Fixpoint distinct (ns : list string) : bool :=
  match ns with [] => true | n :: ns => negb (member n ns) && distinct ns end.

- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

# Split CJR into three local repositories

## Summary

Create three new Git repositories under `/Users/danghica/cjr`:

- **cjr-core**: framework, extraction printers, shared development tools, `rocq_readable`, `misc`, and introductory generics.
- **cjr-collections**: arrays, array lists, polymorphic arrays, linked lists, list reversal, quicksort, most-frequent, hash maps, and quadtrees.
- **cjr-crypto**: AES encryption/decryption and IGE, plus the authenticator core, CLI, and macOS UI.

## Preserve and separate

- Record outstanding exact human prompts using the existing logging script, without duplicate entries.
- First commit the current repository, including new source work, prompt history, and the existing verification log. Do not run proof validation or refresh that log.
- Initialize three fresh Git repositories and record the original checkpoint commit in their migration notes. Do not create remotes or push.
- Preserve source files, fixtures, extracted Cangjie, benchmark history, and required documentation assets; exclude compiled objects, binaries, and caches.
- After confirming migration completeness, remove the original working-tree copies from the parent repository. Retain a lightweight workspace guide, migration records, and historical logs. The checkpoint preserves the monolith.

## Dependencies and tooling

- Retain `example/<name>` within each child repository to reduce path changes.
- Collections depends on core; crypto depends on core and collections. Pin dependency commit IDs, use sibling checkouts by default, and support `CJR_CORE_DIR` and `CJR_COLLECTIONS_DIR` overrides.
- **Keep `rocq_readable` exclusively in cjr-core**, including its implementation, launcher, packaging, renderer resources, and tests. Collections and crypto invoke that copy through their core dependency; neither contains a copied or vendored renderer.
- Keep other shared development tooling and extraction printers in core. Make tooling accept the consuming repository’s root and example inventory.
- Keep arrays and their shared proof tactics in collections. Split extraction drivers and assumption audits by repository, removing circular dependencies and preserving logical module names where possible.
- Provide consistent `build`, `extract`, `test`, `verify`, `docs`, `paper`, `benchmark`, and `report` targets. Separate runtime and document tasks from proof compilation.

## Documentation and benchmarks

- Each repository owns its documentation content, configuration, generated HTML, and explanation caches. Give each a `docs/index.html` linking setup instructions, specifications, tests, benchmarks, and its PDF tutorial.
- Split the tutorial into three independently readable papers with editable LaTeX sources and `paper/tutorial.pdf` outputs. Add missing quadtree and authenticator explanations; preserve qualifications about proof coverage and target-language limitations.
- During migration, relocate existing specification HTML and caches, repair navigation, and avoid invoking proof-checking renderer backends. Future regeneration uses the renderer in core.
- Preserve historical benchmark measurements and provenance. Run fresh collections and AES benchmarks.
- Add core benchmarks for arithmetic, swap, block summation, and generic callbacks against equivalent handwritten Cangjie.
- Add authenticator benchmarks for SHA-1, HMAC, HOTP/TOTP, Base32 decoding, and account-session operations using deterministic synthetic inputs.
- Produce raw samples, medians/IQR, provenance, charts, and HTML reports. Generate each paper’s benchmark material from its repository’s results.

## Validation and completion

- Confirm every original source and fixture has a destination, including uncommitted generics work.
- Check that only core contains the `rocq_readable` implementation and that consumers resolve it through their pinned dependency.
- Run applicable tooling tests, Cangjie runtime tests, benchmark correctness checks, and frontend checks that do not trigger proof validation.
- Build and visually inspect all three PDFs; check HTML links, offline navigation, dependency resolution, and execution without the former monolithic layout.
- Do not run proof compilation, `coqchk`, assumption audits, or proof-checking documentation backends. Preserve existing frontend proof gates and report any validation they prevent.
- Commit each completed child repository and record its commit ID in the parent workspace.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

# Split CJR into three local repositories

## Summary

Create three new Git repositories under `/Users/danghica/cjr`:

- **cjr-core**: framework, extraction printers, shared development tools, `rocq_readable`, `misc`, and introductory generics.
- **cjr-collections**: arrays, array lists, polymorphic arrays, linked lists, list reversal, quicksort, most-frequent, hash maps, and quadtrees.
- **cjr-crypto**: AES encryption/decryption and IGE, plus the authenticator core, CLI, and macOS UI.

## Preserve and separate

- Record outstanding exact human prompts using the existing logging script, without duplicate entries.
- First commit the current repository, including new source work, prompt history, and the existing verification log. Do not run proof validation or refresh that log.
- Initialize three fresh Git repositories and record the original checkpoint commit in their migration notes. Do not create remotes or push.

- After confirming migration completeness, remove the original working-tree copies from the parent repository. Retain a lightweight workspace guide, migration records, and historical logs. The checkpoint preserves the monolith.

- Retain `example/<name>` within each child repository to reduce path changes.
- Collections depends on core; crypto depends on core and collections. Pin dependency commit IDs, use sibling checkouts by default, and support `CJR_CORE_DIR` and `CJR_COLLECTIONS_DIR` overrides.
- **Keep `rocq_readable` exclusively in cjr-core**, including its implementation, launcher, packaging, renderer resources, and tests. Collections and crypto invoke that copy through their core dependency; neither contains a copied or vendored renderer.
- Keep other shared development tooling and extraction printers in core. Make tooling accept the consuming repository’s root and example inventory.
- Keep arrays and their shared proof tactics in collections. Split extraction drivers and assumption audits by repository, removing circular dependencies and preserving logical module names where possible.
- Provide consistent `build`, `extract`, `test`, `verify`, `docs`, `paper`, `benchmark`, and `report` targets. Separate runtime and document tasks from proof compilation.

# Split CJR into three local repositories

## Summary

Create three new Git repositories under `/Users/danghica/cjr`:

- **cjr-core**: framework, extraction printers, shared development tools, `rocq_readable`, `misc`, and introductory generics.
- **cjr-collections**: arrays, array lists, polymorphic arrays, linked lists, list reversal, quicksort, most-frequent, hash maps, and quadtrees.
- **cjr-crypto**: AES encryption/decryption and IGE, plus the authenticator core, CLI, and macOS UI.

Each repository gets offline HTML documentation, a PDF tutorial, and runtime benchmarks. Skip proof validation throughout the migration.

## Preserve and separate

- Record outstanding exact human prompts using the existing logging script, without duplicate entries.
- First commit the current repository, including new source work, prompt history, and the existing verification log. Do not run proof validation or refresh that log.
- Initialize three fresh Git repositories and record the original checkpoint commit in their migration notes. Do not create remotes or push.
- Preserve source files, fixtures, extracted Cangjie, benchmark history, and required documentation assets; exclude compiled objects, binaries, and caches.
- After confirming migration completeness, remove the original working-tree copies from the parent repository. Retain a lightweight workspace guide, migration records, and historical logs. The checkpoint preserves the monolith.

## Dependencies and tooling

- Retain `example/<name>` within each child repository to reduce path changes.
- Collections depends on core; crypto depends on core and collections. Pin dependency commit IDs, use sibling checkouts by default, and support `CJR_CORE_DIR` and `CJR_COLLECTIONS_DIR` overrides.
- **Keep `rocq_readable` exclusively in cjr-core**, including its implementation, launcher, packaging, renderer resources, and tests. Collections and crypto invoke that copy through their core dependency; neither contains a copied or vendored renderer.
- Keep other shared development tooling and extraction printers in core. Make tooling accept the consuming repository’s root and example inventory.
- Keep arrays and their shared proof tactics in collections. Split extraction drivers and assumption audits by repository, removing circular dependencies and preserving logical module names where possible.
- Provide consistent `build`, `extract`, `test`, `verify`, `docs`, `paper`, `benchmark`, and `report` targets. Separate runtime and document tasks from proof compilation.

## Documentation and benchmarks

- Each repository owns its documentation content, configuration, generated HTML, and explanation caches. Give each a `docs/index.html` linking setup instructions, specifications, tests, benchmarks, and its PDF tutorial.
- Split the tutorial into three independently readable papers with editable LaTeX sources and `paper/tutorial.pdf` outputs. Add missing quadtree and authenticator explanations; preserve qualifications about proof coverage and target-language limitations.
- During migration, relocate existing specification HTML and caches, repair navigation, and avoid invoking proof-checking renderer backends. Future regeneration uses the renderer in core.
- Preserve historical benchmark measurements and provenance. Run fresh collections and AES benchmarks.
- Add core benchmarks for arithmetic, swap, block summation, and generic callbacks against equivalent handwritten Cangjie.
- Add authenticator benchmarks for SHA-1, HMAC, HOTP/TOTP, Base32 decoding, and account-session operations using deterministic synthetic inputs.
- Produce raw samples, medians/IQR, provenance, charts, and HTML reports. Generate each paper’s benchmark material from its repository’s results.

## Validation and completion

- Confirm every original source and fixture has a destination, including uncommitted generics work.
- Check that only core contains the `rocq_readable` implementation and that consumers resolve it through their pinned dependency.
- Run applicable tooling tests, Cangjie runtime tests, benchmark correctness checks, and frontend checks that do not trigger proof validation.
- Build and visually inspect all three PDFs; check HTML links, offline navigation, dependency resolution, and execution without the former monolithic layout.
- Do not run proof compilation, `coqchk`, assumption audits, or proof-checking documentation backends. Preserve existing frontend proof gates and report any validation they prevent.
- Commit each completed child repository and record its commit ID in the parent workspace.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

# Split CJR into three local repositories

## Summary

Create three new Git repositories under `/Users/danghica/cjr`:

- **cjr-core**: framework, extraction printers, shared development tools, `rocq_readable`, `misc`, and introductory generics.
- **cjr-collections**: arrays, array lists, polymorphic arrays, linked lists, list reversal, quicksort, most-frequent, hash maps, and quadtrees.
- **cjr-crypto**: AES encryption/decryption and IGE, plus the authenticator core, CLI, and macOS UI.

Each repository gets offline HTML documentation, a PDF tutorial, and runtime benchmarks. Skip proof validation throughout the migration.

## Preserve and separate

- Record outstanding exact human prompts using the existing logging script, without duplicate entries.
- First commit the current repository, including new source work, prompt history, and the existing verification log. Do not run proof validation or refresh that log.
- Initialize three fresh Git repositories and record the original checkpoint commit in their migration notes. Do not create remotes or push.
- Preserve source files, fixtures, extracted Cangjie, benchmark history, and required documentation assets; exclude compiled objects, binaries, and caches.
- After confirming migration completeness, remove the original working-tree copies from the parent repository. Retain a lightweight workspace guide, migration records, and historical logs. The checkpoint preserves the monolith.

## Dependencies and tooling

- Retain `example/<name>` within each child repository to reduce path changes.
- Collections depends on core; crypto depends on core and collections. Pin dependency commit IDs, use sibling checkouts by default, and support `CJR_CORE_DIR` and `CJR_COLLECTIONS_DIR` overrides.
- **Keep `rocq_readable` exclusively in cjr-core**, including its implementation, launcher, packaging, renderer resources, and tests. Collections and crypto invoke that copy through their core dependency; neither contains a copied or vendored renderer.
- Keep other shared development tooling and extraction printers in core. Make tooling accept the consuming repository’s root and example inventory.
- Keep arrays and their shared proof tactics in collections. Split extraction drivers and assumption audits by repository, removing circular dependencies and preserving logical module names where possible.
- Provide consistent `build`, `extract`, `test`, `verify`, `docs`, `paper`, `benchmark`, and `report` targets. Separate runtime and document tasks from proof compilation.

## Documentation and benchmarks

- Each repository owns its documentation content, configuration, generated HTML, and explanation caches. Give each a `docs/index.html` linking setup instructions, specifications, tests, benchmarks, and its PDF tutorial.
- Split the tutorial into three independently readable papers with editable LaTeX sources and `paper/tutorial.pdf` outputs. Add missing quadtree and authenticator explanations; preserve qualifications about proof coverage and target-language limitations.
- During migration, relocate existing specification HTML and caches, repair navigation, and avoid invoking proof-checking renderer backends. Future regeneration uses the renderer in core.
- Preserve historical benchmark measurements and provenance. Run fresh collections and AES benchmarks.
- Add core benchmarks for arithmetic, swap, block summation, and generic callbacks against equivalent handwritten Cangjie.
- Add authenticator benchmarks for SHA-1, HMAC, HOTP/TOTP, Base32 decoding, and account-session operations using deterministic synthetic inputs.
- Produce raw samples, medians/IQR, provenance, charts, and HTML reports. Generate each paper’s benchmark material from its repository’s results.

## Validation and completion

- Confirm every original source and fixture has a destination, including uncommitted generics work.
- Check that only core contains the `rocq_readable` implementation and that consumers resolve it through their pinned dependency.
- Run applicable tooling tests, Cangjie runtime tests, benchmark correctness checks, and frontend checks that do not trigger proof validation.
- Build and visually inspect all three PDFs; check HTML links, offline navigation, dependency resolution, and execution without the former monolithic layout.
- Do not run proof compilation, `coqchk`, assumption audits, or proof-checking documentation backends. Preserve existing frontend proof gates and report any validation they prevent.
- Commit each completed child repository and record its commit ID in the parent workspace.

Acceptance requires three separate repositories with complete HTML documentation, PDF tutorials, and reproducible runtime benchmark reports. Migration notes explicitly state that proofs were not revalidated.

definition local_identifier:
    parameter(ds, type(list(glayout)))
    parameter(fs, type(list(gfunction)))
    parameter(scope, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(n, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        boolean_and:
            identifier(n)
            negb(member(n, append(map(gl_name, ds), append(map(gf_name, fs), scope))))

Depends on

  • cjr.examples.generic_print.identifier
  • cjr.examples.generic_print.member
  • cjr.generics.gfunction
  • cjr.generics.glayout
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition local_identifier (ds : list glayout) (fs : list gfunction) scope n :=
  identifier n && negb (member n (map gl_name ds ++ map gf_name fs ++ scope)).

check_expr

cjr.examples.generic_print.check_expr

AI-generated explanation

Explanation unavailable: No matching cached explanation (offline mode).

recursive function check_expr:
    parameter(ds, type(list(glayout)))
    parameter(fs, type(list(gfunction)))
    parameter(scope, locals, type(list(string)))
    parameter(e, type(gexpr))
    type:
        list(string)
    body:
        match RegularStyle:
            e
            case:
                patterns(GECore(_))
                list("legacy core embedding: use the legacy printer")
            case:
                patterns(GEVal(LitV(LitInt(_))))
                patterns(GEVal(LitV(LitBool(_))))
                patterns(GEVal(LitV(LitUnit)))
                empty_list
            case:
                patterns(GEVal(_))
                list("runtime value has no generic source literal")
            case:
                patterns(GEVar(x))
                require:
                    member(x, locals)
                    notation "_ ++ _":
                        "unbound local: "
                        x
            case:
                patterns(GERef(_, _))
                list:
                    "bare named function reference: pass an explicitly typed lambda instead"
            case:
                patterns(GELam(x, a, b, e))
                notation "_ ++ _":
                    require:
                        local_identifier(ds, fs, scope, x)
                        notation "_ ++ _":
                            "invalid or shadowing lambda parameter: "
                            x
                    append:
                        type_errors(ds, scope, a)
                        append:
                            type_errors(ds, scope, b)
                            check_expr(ds, fs, scope, cons(x, locals), e)
            case:
                patterns(GEApp(a, b))
                append:
                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, a)
                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, b)
            case:
                patterns(GECall(n, ts, es))
                append:
                    match RegularStyle:
                        glookup(n, function_env(fs))
                        case:
                            patterns(None)
                            list:
                                notation "_ ++ _":
                                    "unresolved function (declare dependencies first): "
                                    n
                        case:
                            patterns(Some(f))
                            notation "_ ++ _":
                                require:
                                    match RegularStyle:
                                        gf_owner(f)
                                        case:
                                            patterns(None)
                                            true
                                        case:
                                            patterns(_)
                                            false
                                    notation "_ ++ _":
                                        if_then_else:
                                            gf_static(f)
                                            "static method requires a type-qualified call: "
                                            "method requires a receiver: "
                                        n
                                notation "_ ++ _":
                                    require:
                                        Nat.eqb(length(ts), length(gf_tparams(f)))
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "wrong type arity: "
                                            n
                                    require:
                                        Nat.eqb(length(es), length(gf_params(f)))
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "wrong argument arity: "
                                            n
                    append:
                        flat_map(type_errors(ds, scope), ts)
                        flat_map(check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals), es)
            case:
                patterns(GEMethod(t, r, n, ts, es))
                append:
                    require(nominal(t), "method receiver needs a named type")
                    append:
                        type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                        append:
                            match RegularStyle:
                                glookup(method_key(owner_name(t), n), function_env(fs))
                                case:
                                    patterns(None)
                                    list:
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "unresolved method: "
                                            method_key(owner_name(t), n)
                                case:
                                    patterns(Some(f))
                                    notation "_ ++ _":
                                        require:
                                            negb(gf_static(f))
                                            notation "_ ++ _":
                                                "static method requires a type-qualified call: "
                                                n
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            require:
                                                Nat.eqb(length(ts), length(gf_tparams(f)))
                                                notation "_ ++ _":
                                                    "wrong method type arity: "
                                                    n
                                            require:
                                                Nat.eqb(S(length(es)), length(gf_params(f)))
                                                notation "_ ++ _":
                                                    "wrong method argument arity: "
                                                    n
                            append:
                                flat_map(type_errors(ds, scope), ts)
                                append:
                                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, r)
                                    flat_map(check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals), es)
            case:
                patterns(GEStaticCall(t, n, ts, es))
                append:
                    require(nominal(t), "static method owner needs a named type")
                    append:
                        type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                        append:
                            match RegularStyle:
                                glookup(method_key(owner_name(t), n), function_env(fs))
                                case:
                                    patterns(None)
                                    list:
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "unresolved static method: "
                                            method_key(owner_name(t), n)
                                case:
                                    patterns(Some(f))
                                    notation "_ ++ _":
                                        require:
                                            gf_static(f)
                                            notation "_ ++ _":
                                                "instance method requires a receiver: "
                                                n
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            require:
                                                Nat.eqb(length(ts), length(gf_tparams(f)))
                                                notation "_ ++ _":
                                                    "wrong static method type arity: "
                                                    n
                                            require:
                                                Nat.eqb(length(es), length(gf_params(f)))
                                                notation "_ ++ _":
                                                    "wrong static method argument arity: "
                                                    n
                            append:
                                flat_map(type_errors(ds, scope), ts)
                                flat_map(check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals), es)
            case:
                patterns(GEConstruct(t, es))
                append:
                    require(nominal(t), "constructor needs a named type")
                    append:
                        type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                        append:
                            match RegularStyle:
                                glookup(owner_name(t), layout_env(ds))
                                case:
                                    patterns(None)
                                    list:
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "unresolved constructor: "
                                            owner_name(t)
                                case:
                                    patterns(Some(d))
                                    require:
                                        Nat.eqb(length(es), length(gl_fields(d)))
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "wrong constructor arity: "
                                            owner_name(t)
                            flat_map(check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals), es)
            case:
                patterns(GEField(t, r, i))
                append:
                    require(nominal(t), "field receiver needs a named type")
                    append:
                        type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                        append:
                            match RegularStyle:
                                glookup(owner_name(t), layout_env(ds))
                                case:
                                    patterns(None)
                                    list:
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "unresolved field owner: "
                                            owner_name(t)
                                case:
                                    patterns(Some(d))
                                    require:
                                        Nat.ltb(i, length(gl_fields(d)))
                                        notation "_ ++ _":
                                            "field index out of range: "
                                            owner_name(t)
                            check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, r)
            case:
                patterns(GELet(x, a, b))
                append:
                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, a)
                    match RegularStyle:
                        x
                        case:
                            patterns(None)
                            check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, b)
                        case:
                            patterns(Some(x))
                            notation "_ ++ _":
                                require:
                                    local_identifier(ds, fs, scope, x)
                                    notation "_ ++ _":
                                        "invalid or shadowing local: "
                                        x
                                check_expr(ds, fs, scope, cons(x, locals), b)
            case:
                patterns(GEIf(c, a, b))
                append:
                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, c)
                    append:
                        check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, a)
                        check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, b)
            case:
                patterns(GEBinOp(_, a, b))
                append:
                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, a)
                    check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, b)
            case:
                patterns(GEBufferMake(t, a, b))
                patterns(GEBufferGet(t, a, b))
                append:
                    type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                    append:
                        check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, a)
                        check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, b)
            case:
                patterns(GEBufferSet(t, a, b, c))
                append:
                    type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                    append:
                        check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, a)
                        append:
                            check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, b)
                            check_expr(ds, fs, scope, locals, c)

Original declaration (proof-free)
Fixpoint check_expr (ds : list glayout) (fs : list gfunction)
    (scope locals : list string) (e : gexpr) : list string :=
  match e with
  | GECore _ => ["legacy core embedding: use the legacy printer"]
  | GEVal (LitV (LitInt _)) | GEVal (LitV (LitBool _)) | GEVal (LitV LitUnit) => []
  | GEVal _ => ["runtime value has no generic source literal"]
  | GEVar x => require (member x locals) ("unbound local: " ++ x)
  | GERef _ _ => ["bare named function reference: pass an explicitly typed lambda instead"]
  | GELam x a b e =>
      require (local_identifier ds fs scope x) ("invalid or shadowing lambda parameter: " ++ x) ++
      type_errors ds scope a ++ type_errors ds scope b ++ check_expr ds fs scope (x :: locals) e
  | GEApp a b => check_expr ds fs scope locals a ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals b
  | GECall n ts es =>
      (match glookup n (function_env fs) with
       | None => ["unresolved function (declare dependencies first): " ++ n]
       | Some f => require (match gf_owner f with None => true | _ => false end)
           ((if gf_static f then "static method requires a type-qualified call: "
             else "method requires a receiver: ") ++ n) ++
           require (Nat.eqb (length ts) (length (gf_tparams f))) ("wrong type arity: " ++ n) ++
           require (Nat.eqb (length es) (length (gf_params f))) ("wrong argument arity: " ++ n)
       end) ++ flat_map (type_errors ds scope) ts ++
       flat_map (check_expr ds fs scope locals) es
  | GEMethod t r n ts es =>
      require (nominal t) "method receiver needs a named type" ++ type_errors ds scope t ++
      (match glookup (method_key (owner_name t) n) (function_env fs) with
       | None => ["unresolved method: " ++ method_key (owner_name t) n]
       | Some f => require (negb (gf_static f)) ("static method requires a type-qualified call: " ++ n) ++
          require (Nat.eqb (length ts) (length (gf_tparams f))) ("wrong method type arity: " ++ n) ++
          require (Nat.eqb (S (length es)) (length (gf_params f))) ("wrong method argument arity: " ++ n)
       end) ++ flat_map (type_errors ds scope) ts ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals r ++
       flat_map (check_expr ds fs scope locals) es
  | GEStaticCall t n ts es =>
      require (nominal t) "static method owner needs a named type" ++ type_errors ds scope t ++
      (match glookup (method_key (owner_name t) n) (function_env fs) with
       | None => ["unresolved static method: " ++ method_key (owner_name t) n]
       | Some f => require (gf_static f) ("instance method requires a receiver: " ++ n) ++
          require (Nat.eqb (length ts) (length (gf_tparams f))) ("wrong static method type arity: " ++ n) ++
          require (Nat.eqb (length es) (length (gf_params f))) ("wrong static method argument arity: " ++ n)
       end) ++ flat_map (type_errors ds scope) ts ++
       flat_map (check_expr ds fs scope locals) es
  | GEConstruct t es =>
      require (nominal t) "constructor needs a named type" ++ type_errors ds scope t ++
      (match glookup (owner_name t) (layout_env ds) with
       | None => ["unresolved constructor: " ++ owner_name t]
       | Some d => require (Nat.eqb (length es) (length (gl_fields d)))
           ("wrong constructor arity: " ++ owner_name t)
       end) ++ flat_map (check_expr ds fs scope locals) es
  | GEField t r i =>
      require (nominal t) "field receiver needs a named type" ++ type_errors ds scope t ++
      (match glookup (owner_name t) (layout_env ds) with
       | None => ["unresolved field owner: " ++ owner_name t]
       | Some d => require (Nat.ltb i (length (gl_fields d))) ("field index out of range: " ++ owner_name t)
       end) ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals r
  | GELet x a b => check_expr ds fs scope locals a ++
      (match x with
       | None => check_expr ds fs scope locals b
       | Some x => require (local_identifier ds fs scope x) ("invalid or shadowing local: " ++ x) ++ check_expr ds fs scope (x :: locals) b
       end)
  | GEIf c a b => check_expr ds fs scope locals c ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals a ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals b
  | GEBinOp _ a b => check_expr ds fs scope locals a ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals b
  | GEBufferMake t a b | GEBufferGet t a b =>
      type_errors ds scope t ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals a ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals b
  | GEBufferSet t a b c => type_errors ds scope t ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals a ++
      check_expr ds fs scope locals b ++ check_expr ds fs scope locals c
  end.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

# Split CJR into three local repositories

## Summary

Create three new Git repositories under `/Users/danghica/cjr`:

- **cjr-core**: framework, extraction printers, shared development tools, `rocq_readable`, `misc`, and introductory generics.
- **cjr-collections**: arrays, array lists, polymorphic arrays, linked lists, list reversal, quicksort, most-frequent, hash maps, and quadtrees.
- **cjr-crypto**: AES encryption/decryption and IGE, plus the authenticator core, CLI, and macOS UI.

Each repository gets offline HTML documentation, a PDF tutorial, and runtime benchmarks. Skip proof validation throughout the migration.

## Preserve and separate

- Record outstanding exact human prompts using the existing logging script, without duplicate entries.
- First commit the current repository, including new source work, prompt history, and the existing verification log. Do not run proof validation or refresh that log.
- Initialize three fresh Git repositories and record the original checkpoint commit in their migration notes. Do not create remotes or push.
- Preserve source files, fixtures, extracted Cangjie, benchmark history, and required documentation assets; exclude compiled objects, binaries, and caches.
- After confirming migration completeness, remove the original working-tree copies from the parent repository. Retain a lightweight workspace guide, migration records, and historical logs. The checkpoint preserves the monolith.

## Dependencies and tooling

- Retain `example/<name>` within each child repository to reduce path changes.
- Collections depends on core; crypto depends on core and collections. Pin dependency commit IDs, use sibling checkouts by default, and support `CJR_CORE_DIR` and `CJR_COLLECTIONS_DIR` overrides.
- **Keep `rocq_readable` exclusively in cjr-core**, including its implementation, launcher, packaging, renderer resources, and tests. Collections and crypto invoke that copy through their core dependency; neither contains a copied or vendored renderer.
- Keep other shared development tooling and extraction printers in core. Make tooling accept the consuming repository’s root and example inventory.
- Keep arrays and their shared proof tactics in collections. Split extraction drivers and assumption audits by repository, removing circular dependencies and preserving logical module names where possible.
- Provide consistent `build`, `extract`, `test`, `verify`, `docs`, `paper`, `benchmark`, and `report` targets. Separate runtime and document tasks from proof compilation.

## Documentation and benchmarks

- Each repository owns its documentation content, configuration, generated HTML, and explanation caches. Give each a `docs/index.html` linking setup instructions, specifications, tests, benchmarks, and its PDF tutorial.
- Split the tutorial into three independently readable papers with editable LaTeX sources and `paper/tutorial.pdf` outputs. Add missing quadtree and authenticator explanations; preserve qualifications about proof coverage and target-language limitations.
- During migration, relocate existing specification HTML and caches, repair navigation, and avoid invoking proof-checking renderer backends. Future regeneration uses the renderer in core.
- Preserve historical benchmark measurements and provenance. Run fresh collections and AES benchmarks.
- Add core benchmarks for arithmetic, swap, block summation, and generic callbacks against equivalent handwritten Cangjie.
- Add authenticator benchmarks for SHA-1, HMAC, HOTP/TOTP, Base32 decoding, and account-session operations using deterministic synthetic inputs.
- Produce raw samples, medians/IQR, provenance, charts, and HTML reports. Generate each paper’s benchmark material from its repository’s results.

## Validation and completion

- Confirm every original source and fixture has a destination, including uncommitted generics work.
- Check that only core contains the `rocq_readable` implementation and that consumers resolve it through their pinned dependency.
- Run applicable tooling tests, Cangjie runtime tests, benchmark correctness checks, and frontend checks that do not trigger proof validation.
- Build and visually inspect all three PDFs; check HTML links, offline navigation, dependency resolution, and execution without the former monolithic layout.
- Do not run proof compilation, `coqchk`, assumption audits, or proof-checking documentation backends. Preserve existing frontend proof gates and report any validation they prevent.
- Commit each completed child repository and record its commit ID in the parent workspace.

Acceptance requires three separate repositories with complete HTML documentation, PDF tutorials, and reproducible runtime benchmark reports. Migration notes explicitly state that proofs were not revalidated.

PLEASE IMPLEMENT THIS PLAN:
# Parametric polymorphism for CJR

## Summary

Add an explicitly parameterised source language that supports generic functions, classes, structs, and methods. Erase it into the existing CJR operational language for Iris verification, and print it directly as Cangjie generics.

Demonstrate the extension with generic identity, `Pair<A, B>`, and `Box<T>` with `map<U>`.

## Implementation

- Add structured source types for primitives, type variables, named type applications, and function types. Define scoped type parameters, substitution, instantiation, and well-formedness checks.
- Add generic declarations and source expressions with explicit declaration references, constructor names, receiver types, and type arguments. Support rank-one polymorphism and callbacks with monomorphic function types.
- Define erasure into existing CJR expressions: type arguments disappear, named calls resolve to existing function terms, and constructors and fields resolve to their declared layouts. Preserve evaluation order and existing recursive-function behavior.
- Prove that type instantiation preserves erasure and that erasure commutes with supported term substitution. Express generic weakest preconditions through erasure and derive safety using existing CJR adequacy.
- Extend extraction with a structured generic printing path. Use explicit references rather than body equality or field-count heuristics. Reject unresolved names, unbound type variables, incorrect arities, and unsupported forms with actionable diagnostics.
- Preserve existing extraction interfaces and monomorphic examples. Add the generic example to aggregate extraction, runtime testing, documentation, and the assumption audit.

## Examples and proof obligations

- **Identity:** prove that the same implementation returns its input uniformly for any element representation.
- **Pair:** prove construction and projections for independently parameterised components.
- **Box:** prove construction, reading, and `map<U>`, with a callback contract relating input and output representations. State ownership requirements explicitly; do not assume arbitrary Iris resources can be duplicated.
- Instantiate examples with `Int64`, `Bool`, and nested generic values. Include a method whose own type parameter differs from its class parameter.

## Validation

- Check substitution, nested instantiation, type-parameter scoping, and erasure laws in Rocq.
- Test extraction failures for invalid references and arities, and disambiguation of declarations with identical bodies or layouts.
- Compile and execute generated generic examples using the repository’s Cangjie 1.0.5 toolchain.
- Run existing runtime suites and extraction regressions, then `make verify`; audit the new public proof results without admissions or added axioms.
- Update the README and tutorial to explain generic syntax, Iris contracts, erasure, and extraction’s remaining trust boundary.

## Assumptions and boundaries

- Keep the current untyped runtime and existing proofs stable. This extension provides uniform Iris specifications, not a general typing-soundness or relational-parametricity theorem.
- Defer interface bounds, inheritance, higher-rank polymorphism, generic raw-memory allocation, and migration of existing containers.
- During implementation, record outstanding human prompts exactly through the repository logging helper, without duplicating existing entries. Preserve existing log changes and refresh the verification log.

cjr.examples.generic_print.check_function

Explanation unavailable: No matching cached explanation (offline mode).

definition check_function:
    parameter(ds, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(previous, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(f, type(_ (inferred)))
    type:
        list(string)
    body:
        let:
            scope
            in_scope list(append(owner_parameters(ds, f), gf_tparams(f)))
            notation "_ ++ _":
                require:
                    identifier(gf_name(f))
                    notation "_ ++ _":
                        "invalid function name: "
                        gf_name(f)
                append:
                    require:
                        boolean_and(distinct(scope), forallb(identifier, scope))
                        "invalid, shadowed, or duplicate type parameters"
                    append:
                        require:
                            boolean_and:
                                distinct(map(fst, gf_params(f)))
                                forallb:
                                    local_identifier(ds, cons(f, previous), scope)
                                    map(fst, gf_params(f))
                            "invalid or duplicate parameters"
                        append:
                            match RegularStyle:
                                gf_owner(f)
                                case:
                                    patterns(None)
                                    require:
                                        negb(gf_static(f))
                                        "static method needs an owner"
                                case:
                                    patterns(Some(o))
                                    match RegularStyle:
                                        glookup(o, layout_env(ds))
                                        case:
                                            patterns(Some(d))
                                            append:
                                                if_then_else:
                                                    gf_static(f)
                                                    empty_list
                                                    match RegularStyle:
                                                        gf_params(f)
                                                        case:
                                                            patterns:
                                                                cons:
                                                                    tuple_with_tail_items:
                                                                        _
                                                                        items(receiver)
                                                                    _
                                                            require:
                                                                gty_beq:
                                                                    receiver
                                                                    GTNamed:
                                                                        o
                                                                        map:
                                                                            GTVar
                                                                            gl_tparams(d)
                                                                "method first parameter must be its owning type with its type parameters"
                                                        case:
                                                            patterns(empty_list)
                                                            list:
                                                                "unknown method owner or missing receiver"
                                                require:
                                                    negb:
                                                        member:
                                                            gf_name(f)
                                                            map(fst, gl_fields(d))
                                                    "method conflicts with a field"
                                        case:
                                            patterns(None)
                                            list("unknown method owner or missing receiver")
                            append:
                                flat_map:
                                    lambda:
                                        tuple_with_tail_items(_, items(t))
                                        type_errors(ds, scope, t)
                                    gf_params(f)
                                append:
                                    type_errors(ds, scope, gf_ret(f))
                                    check_expr:
                                        ds
                                        cons(f, previous)
                                        scope
                                        map(fst, gf_params(f))
                                        gf_body(f)

Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition check_function ds previous f : list string :=
  let scope := (owner_parameters ds f ++ gf_tparams f)%list in
  require (identifier (gf_name f)) ("invalid function name: " ++ gf_name f) ++
  require (distinct scope && forallb identifier scope) "invalid, shadowed, or duplicate type parameters" ++
  require (distinct (map fst (gf_params f)) && forallb (local_identifier ds (f :: previous) scope) (map fst (gf_params f))) "invalid or duplicate parameters" ++
  (match gf_owner f with
   | None => require (negb (gf_static f)) "static method needs an owner"
   | Some o =>
       match glookup o (layout_env ds) with
       | Some d =>
           (if gf_static f then [] else
            match gf_params f with
            | (_, receiver) :: _ =>
                require (gty_beq receiver (GTNamed o (map GTVar (gl_tparams d))))
                  "method first parameter must be its owning type with its type parameters"
            | [] => ["unknown method owner or missing receiver"]
            end) ++
           require (negb (member (gf_name f) (map fst (gl_fields d)))) "method conflicts with a field"
       | None => ["unknown method owner or missing receiver"]
       end
   end) ++
  flat_map (fun '(_, t) => type_errors ds scope t) (gf_params f) ++ type_errors ds scope (gf_ret f) ++
  check_expr ds (f :: previous) scope (map fst (gf_params f)) (gf_body f).

Fixpoint check_functions ds previous fs : list string :=
  match fs with
  | [] => []
  | f :: fs => check_function ds previous f ++ check_functions ds (f :: previous) fs
